I mean, at work, I just eat my lunch. It's fine. It's just... later on.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty after eating late at night?\n\nPatient: I don't know... I guess I just try not to think about it too much. I usually just... go to sleep after.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever eat a large amount of food in a short period of time?\n\nPatient: I mean... I guess it depends on what you consider a lot. But... yeah. Some nights after my shift, I'll just be standing in the kitchen... acting like I'm just cleaning up the leftovers. But I can end up going through several tamales, a big bowl of pozole... tortillas, whatever rice or beans are still sitting out. It... it goes pretty fast, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're able to stop eating once you've started, during those late-night episodes?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. Um... that's the part that bothers me, I guess. Once I start picking at the food... I try to stop. I'll tell myself, 'That's enough, Santiago.' But... my hand just keeps going back. It feels like... like I don't get to make the decision anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're able to control how much you eat during those late-night episodes?\n\nPatient: Um... like I just said, no. Not really. I try to tell myself that's enough, but... yeah, it just feels like I don't get to make the decision on how much I'm having. I just keep going back.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel upset or distressed after these late-night eating episodes?\n\nPatient: I mean... yeah. A little, I guess. It's just... frustrating.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're not in control of your eating during the day?\n\nPatient: No... not really. During the day I'm fine. I just eat normally, I guess. It's really just... at night.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're able to stop eating once you've started during those episodes because you want to, or do you feel like something else is driving you to continue?\n\nPatient: I mean... like I said a minute ago, I can't really stop. Once I start picking at the food late at night, I'll tell myself, 'That's enough, Santiago.' But... my hand just keeps going back. It just feels like I don't make the decision anymore. I don't know about something else driving me, it's just... I can't stop.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel anxious, stressed, or upset right before these late-night eating episodes?\n\nPatient: I don't know. Not really... I mean, I'm just tired from work, mostly. I haven't really noticed feeling upset or anything like that before it happens.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you eat at night because you're feeling bored, or because of some other emotion?\n\nPatient: I don't think so. I'm not really sitting around bored. And I don't know about... emotions. I just watch TV after my shift and start eating. I don't really think about why.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually eat alone when you have these late-night eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah... I do. I usually wait until my aunt and the rest of the house are asleep. I just... I don't really like anyone seeing how much I put away. So I try to keep it quiet in the kitchen.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're not in control of what you're eating during the day, even if it's just for a moment?\n\nPatient: No, not really. During the day I'm at work, so... I just eat my lunch and get back to the floor. I don't really have a problem then.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you eat at night because you're feeling something, like sadness or happiness?\n\nPatient: I mean... like I said a minute ago, I don't really think it's an emotion thing. I don't feel particularly sad or happy. I'm just... tired from the warehouse, I guess. I don't really pay attention to feelings like that. I just eat.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

    "content": "Return the JSON now."

I haven't really noticed feeling upset or anything like that before it happens.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you eat at night because you're feeling bored, or because of some other emotion?\n\nPatient: I don't think so. I'm not really sitting around bored. And I don't know about... emotions. I just watch TV after my shift and start eating. I don't really think about why.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually eat alone when you have these late-night eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah... I do. I usually wait until my aunt and the rest of the house are asleep. I just... I don't really like anyone seeing how much I put away. So I try to keep it quiet in the kitchen.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're not in control of what you're eating during the day, even if it's just for a moment?\n\nPatient: No, not really. During the day I'm at work, so... I just eat my lunch and get back to the floor. I don't really have a problem then.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you eat at night because you're feeling something, like sadness or happiness?\n\nPatient: I mean... like I said a minute ago, I don't really think it's an emotion thing. I don't feel particularly sad or happy. I'm just... tired from the warehouse, I guess. I don't really pay attention to feelings like that. I just eat.\n\nDoctor: Do you eat at night in secret because you're embarrassed about how much you're eating?\n\nPatient: Yeah... I mean, like I said before. I just... I don't really like anyone seeing how much I put away. That's why I wait until my aunt and everyone else is asleep. It's just easier to keep it quiet in the kitchen and keep it to myself.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel ashamed of yourself after these episodes of eating at night because you couldn't stop?\n\nPatient: I don't know if... I wouldn't really use the word ashamed. I mean, I don't sit around thinking about it after. Once I'm done, I just clean up and go to sleep so I can get up for work. I don't really dwell on it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you could eat a large amount of food in a short time during the day if you wanted to, or is this only happening at night?\n\nPatient: I don't really do that during the day. I'm at work. It's... it's just on some nights, after work. I'll just stand there in the kitchen, looking like I'm just cleaning up the leftovers, you know? And... I guess I can go through a lot then. Several tamales, a big bowl of pozole, tortillas... whatever rice or beans are left in the pans. But yeah, it's just at night.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're eating at night because you feel like you have to, or because you want to?\n\nPatient: I don't really know. I guess... it's not exactly about wanting to. It's just... once I start picking at the food, I'll tell myself, you know, \"That's enough, Santiago.\" But... my hand just keeps going back. It kind of feels like I don't get to make the decision anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're not hungry during the day?\n\nPatient: No... I get hungry. I take my lunch break at the warehouse like normal. I don't really have a problem then.\n\nDoctor: Do you typically skip meals during the day?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really skip meals. Like I said, I eat my lunch. I have breakfast before I head out. It's just normal eating during the day."
